PART 5: LICENCE DATA SOURCES INVENTORY

Scope: sources of licence-holder data only — NOT a list of every licence holder.

5.0 Central Arizona problem

Arizona does not publish one downloadable, bulk, machine-readable register of marijuana licence holders, and it does not publish a current public hemp licensee roster that could be found on the official sites searched. The usable system is: (1) counts only in the ADHS monthly report; (2) record-by-record search only in AZCareCheck for adult-use establishments and testing labs; (3) case-by-case PDFs / agenda files for local permits; (4) aggregate tax only from ADOR — no holder-level tax list; (5) no public roster found for AZDA hemp licensees, for tribal marijuana licensees, or for social-equity licensees as a separate file.

Licence architecture — why counts do not add up: one operator can hold an adult-use establishment licence, a medical DRC, and several cultivation/manufacturing sites, and can appear at one address under both programmes. Do not sum 170 + 136 = 306 locations.

5.1 ADHS — licensee / registrant lists

5.1.1 ADHS Marijuana Licensing hub (portal gateway, NOT a public list). URL: ADHS Marijuana Licensing hub [Directly opened 31 Oct 2026 — page returned only footer/privacy text in text fetch; no licence list, no download link was extractable]. Holder types: marijuana establishments (adult-use), nonprofit medical dispensaries, marijuana testing facilities, facility/dispensary/laboratory agents. Format: web portal hub, not CSV/XLSX/PDF. Access: click through to Facility Licensing Portal and Individual Licensing Portal (see §5.1.4). No public bulk download found. Update frequency: not stated. Fields: none publicly extractable. Limitation: hub does not itself give a searchable holder list in text form; searchable holder source is AZCareCheck (§5.2), count source is monthly report (§5.4). The ADHS Marijuana Licensing landing page itself renders almost no text in a text fetch — it is a JavaScript/portal page — so its sub-links could not be extracted that way; that is a limitation, not proof the links do not exist in a live browser.

5.1.2 ADHS Medical Marijuana hub (portal gateway + allocation link). URL: ADHS Medical Marijuana hub [Directly opened 31 Oct 2026]. Holder types: qualifying patients, designated caregivers, dispensary agents, facility agents, laboratory agents, nonprofit medical dispensaries, laboratories. Format: web navigation page; links to Individual Licensing Portal, Facility Licensing Portal, Reports, Statutes & Rules, Dispensaries, Labs/Lab Agents, Testing Advisory Council, Complaints. Access: free, no login for hub itself; portals login-only. Update frequency: not stated. Latest data: menu item “2026 Nonprofit Medical Marijuana Dispensary Allocation” is listed, but its destination URL was not extracted in text fetch — [Verify direct link in a live browser before citing it]. Fields: none as a holder list. Limitation: no medical dispensary roster is published on this hub page itself; medical counts come only from monthly report (§5.4). Medical licence details are not in AZCareCheck — see §5.2, statutory confidentiality.

5.1.3 Historical ADHS “Licensed Marijuana Establishments” PDF — ARCHIVE ONLY, do not use as current. Direct download — 22 Jan 2021 version: licensed-marijuana-establishments-01.22.2021 (1).pdf — file name licensed-marijuana-establishments-01.22.2021 (1).pdf. Fields: Status (Open/Not [operating]), Certificate Number, Establishment Name, DBA, Street Address, City, Zip Code. Stated on face: “Updated 01.22.2021 4:00pm” and “Total Licensees: 86”. Later mirror copy (same table, undated in search extract, hosted by a third party): pamos.com mirror (PDF) — NOT an official ADHS URL; treat as archive/mirror only, do not cite as current ADHS publication. Limitation: no current (2025–2026) equivalent of this PDF was found on azdhs.gov by search on 31 Oct 2026. Current route for named adult-use establishments is AZCareCheck (§5.2), not a PDF list. Any dossier reproducing the 2021/2023 PDF as current would be wrong by ~84 licences (86 in Jan 2021 vs 170 in Aug 2026, §5.6).

5.1.4 ADHS licensing portals — application/management systems, NOT public searches. Individual Licensing Portal: linked from medical hub as individual-licensing.azdhs.gov (exact path not extracted — use hub link). Facility Licensing Portal: linked as facility-licensing.azdhs.gov (exact path not extracted — use hub link). Holder types: patients, caregivers, dispensary agents, facility agents, laboratory agents (individual portal); dispensaries/establishments/labs as applicants (facility portal). Format: login-only web application. No public search, no CSV/PDF download, no export button for the public. Fields (for holder/applicant only): application status, renewals, changes, card/certificate management. Limitation: cannot be used by a researcher to enumerate holders; included only so the reader does not mistake a portal link for a public register.

5.1.5 ADHS data catalogue entry — “Marijuana Licensing Profile” (metadata, not a holder file). Catalogue API/list: ADHS data catalogue [found via search, not directly opened as a data file]. Profile file linked from catalogue: marijuana-licensing-profile.pdf — file name marijuana-licensing-profile.pdf. Holder types described: “medical marijuana qualified patients, caregivers, facility agents, lab agents, and dispensary agents, as well as medical marijuana dispensaries and adult-use marijuana establishments.” Format: PDF profile describing the data source; Division: Licensing, Bureau: BML. Access: download profile PDF from Drive link; underlying applicant/licensee/complaint/survey/enforcement data itself is not attached — it would require an ADHS records/data request (ADHS hub links “Request Information, Records, or Data”). Update frequency/latest date: not stated in catalogue extract. Limitation: a profile is not a register; do not cite it as a count source.

5.1.6 ADHS Laboratory Licensure & Certification hub — general labs, NOT a marijuana-lab roster. URL: ADHS Lab Licensure & Certification hub [found via search]. Holder types: clinical, environmental and marijuana laboratory testing — page states it “Certif[ies] and regulate[s] laboratories that perform marijuana laboratory tests for the potency and safety of all certified marijuana laboratories in Arizona.” Format: web hub. Access: free. Limitation: no standalone, downloadable list of certified marijuana testing laboratories was found on this hub. Marijuana lab count = monthly report (§5.4); named labs = AZCareCheck (§5.2). Individual lab certificates (e.g. a lab’s own ADHS certificate PDF) are published by labs themselves, not as an ADHS roster — [Verify if a live-browser ADHS lab page exists behind the hub].

5.2 ADHS enforcement / compliance — AZCareCheck (the ONLY current named-holder search)

AZCareCheck — adult-use establishments + testing labs ONLY. URL: AZCareCheck [Directly opened 31 Oct 2026 — returned only “AZ Care Check / Loading” in text fetch, confirming it is a JavaScript app; search/detail functions require a live browser]. Alias: azcarecheck.com redirects to AZCareCheck main page, per 2024 Citation Report (below). Holder types: adult-use marijuana establishments and marijuana testing laboratories. Medical marijuana licences are expressly excluded. Format: interactive searchable database. No bulk CSV/XLSX/PDF download was described in any official source found; no export button is documented — treat as search-only. [Verify export in a live browser; do not assume one exists.] Access/how to search (per 2024 Citation Report): from azdhs.gov go to AZCareCheck → under Facilities select Marijuana → search page → select Marijuana Facilities to see all adult-use establishments and testing labs → select ‘Get Details’ on an entity for inspections and enforcements, if any. Search fields (per AZCareCheck general description, third-party summary of ADHS): facility/provider name, address, licence type/subtype, licensing status [Verify field list in live browser for the marijuana subset]. Fields on a detail record (as described): licensing history, which may include deficiencies and/or enforcement actions; inspections and enforcements if they exist. Update frequency as stated: “maintained and updated daily by ADHS and not by an outside source” and “Primary Source Verified” — 2024 Citation Report, p.4. Latest data date: no dated snapshot; it is a live database, so “latest date” is the date of the search. Limitations — critical: Medical exclusion: “Citations and enforcements against medical marijuana licenses are not available on AZCareCheck, as they are protected under A.R.S. § 36-2810(A)(2).” — 2024 Citation Report. No social-equity flag, no delivery flag, no cultivation-site vs retail-site split was documented in sources found — [Verify whether AZCareCheck detail pages carry those fields before using it to filter social-equity or delivery holders]. No bulk download means AZCareCheck cannot, on its own, be turned into a complete holder list without record-by-record extraction (and any such extraction must be dated, because the database changes daily).

ADHS Bureau of Marijuana Licensing — Citation Findings 2024 (aggregate enforcement, with AZCareCheck instructions). Direct download: 2024-citation-report-overview.pdf — file name 2024-citation-report-overview.pdf (query string ?v=20250909 is a version stamp, not part of file name). Holder types: adult-use establishments and medical dispensaries, broken down by site type: establishment cultivation, manufacturing site, dispensary cultivation, retail site. Format: PDF report, not a holder list. No CSV/XLSX companion found. Access: direct PDF download, free. Update frequency: annual report (2024 edition; version stamp Sept 2025). No 2025 edition found on 31 Oct 2026 — [Verify whether a 2025 citation report exists]. Latest data date: calendar year 2024; baseline counts on p.2: “As of December 31, 2024, there were 135 operating medical marijuana dispensary licenses and 168 operating adult-use establishment licenses.” Fields/contents: top citations by rule (A.A.C. R9-18 and R9-17, A.R.S. Title 36), counts by site type, and AZCareCheck instructions in §3.0. Key aggregate figures (directly opened): 603 citations issued to 188 licences in 2024 (dispensary + establishment combined); 68 citations resulted in enforcement; plans of correction submitted and accepted for each citation. Breakdown: establishment cultivation 96 citations / 32 licences; manufacturing sites 26 / 11; dispensary cultivations 196 / 45; retail locations 284 / 141. Limitations: aggregate only — it does not name cited holders. Named enforcement requires AZCareCheck. It does not separate social-equity, delivery, or tribal holders.

5.3 ADHS product recalls and health advisories — individual notices only, NO consolidated database found

Channel: ADHS news releases via GovDelivery (content.govdelivery.com, account AZDHS). There is no consolidated recall register, CSV, or searchable recall database found on azdhs.gov. Format: individual HTML news-release bulletins, each with a product table. No bulk download. Access: free; bulletins found by searching ADHS GovDelivery / ADHS news for “Voluntary recall of certain marijuana products.” Update frequency: ad hoc, per incident. No stated schedule. Latest examples found (by search, not as a complete list): 18 Dec 2025 — voluntary recall for possible Spinosad pesticide contamination, following a routine lab inspection at Kaycha Labs where failed Spinosad tests were reported as passing: GovDelivery bulletin 400259d; undated in extract, Aspergillus/Salmonella recalls (multiple bulletins, e.g.): GovDelivery bulletin 2e53d1c; 2025 Aspergillus recalls reported in third-party republication of ADHS notices (e.g. Legacy & Co / Nature’s Wonder batches) — [Verify dates and bulletin URLs against GovDelivery; third-party republications are not the official record]. Fields in each bulletin table: License Holder/Cultivator, Brand Name, Product Name, Batch Number, Product Type, Implicated Contaminant (Aspergillus spp., Salmonella, Spinosad in examples found). Limitations — critical: ADHS itself states (Director’s Blog explainer, “Marijuana product recalls: Here’s how they happen,” Director’s Blog) that a recall notice includes cultivator name, product name, product type and batch number, but dispensary names selling the product are NOT included because the list may not be comprehensive. Recalls to date in that explainer are described as for Aspergillus and Salmonella; the Spinosad example shows the contaminant set has since widened — do not close the list at those two. No separate “health advisory” index distinct from recall bulletins was found. If a health-advisory series is needed, it must be built bulletin-by-bulletin from GovDelivery and dated as such — [Verify whether ADHS maintains a separate advisories page in a live browser].

5.4 ADHS medical marijuana reports and patient statistics (counts only)

Arizona Marijuana Program Monthly Report (the authoritative count source). Landing/hub: via Medical Marijuana hub (§5.1.2) → “Reports.” Individual month PDFs follow a stable pattern (see below); no single index page URL with all months was extracted in text form. Format: PDF only. No CSV/XLSX/dashboard version found. Access: direct PDF download per month, free. Update frequency: monthly. Tables 1–4 are point-in-time; Tables 5–7, 10, 13 and Figure 1 are for the report month; Tables 5, 8–10, 14 and Figure 2 are cumulative for the calendar year (wording as in July 2026 report). Direct downloads verified by search/open: July 2026 (directly opened 31 Oct 2026): mm-jul26.pdf; April 2026: mm-apr26.pdf; February 2026: mm-feb26.pdf; September 2025: mm-sep25.pdf; December 2024: mm-dec24.pdf; October 2024: mm-oct24.pdf; May 2024 / March 2024: same /reports/2024/mm-may24.pdf and mm-mar24.pdf pattern [found via search]. Fields (July 2026 report, directly opened): active cardholders by type (qualifying patients, designated caregivers, dispensary agents, facility agents, laboratory agents); patients by age/sex; patients by qualifying condition (chronic pain dominant); cardholders by county of residence; applications by type (new/renewal/changed) and by county; revoked cards; physicians providing certifications; medical dispensary transaction summary in ounces/pounds (month and year-to-date); “Current Establishments” and “Current Labs” sentences (see §5.6); medical dispensary total/operating count. Latest data date found: July 2026 report, with establishment/dispensary/lab counts stated “As of 8/1/2026” (see §5.6). ADOR landing page (§5.5) is headed September 2026, but no August or September 2026 ADHS monthly report PDF was directly opened on 31 Oct 2026 — [Verify whether an August/September 2026 ADHS monthly report exists; do not assume July figures are still current in October]. Limitations: counts only — no holder names, addresses, or licence numbers in monthly report. Adult-use sales weight/transactions are NOT in this report — it covers medical dispensary transactions only; adult-use volume must come from ADOR taxable sales (§5.5), and the two series cannot be added together (different bases: ADHS = medical ounces/transactions; ADOR = taxable sales estimated from tax received). Point-in-time date ambiguity: report says Tables 1–4 are point-in-time but does not date them on the face of the extract, while establishment/dispensary/lab sentences are stamped 1st of the following month — cite the 1 Aug 2026 stamp for licence counts, not an assumed July cut-off for cardholder tables. Agent-card figures in the July 2026 text extraction are garbled in places (e.g. “Dispensary Agents 2” row) — use clearly stated totals (patients, total cardholders, dispensaries, establishments, labs) and [Verify agent-card splits from the PDF visually before reproducing them].

ADHS annual/historical reports. Format: PDF reports (e.g. Medical Marijuana Year 3 Annual Report, discussed on Director’s Blog: Director’s Blog Year 3 report). Limitations: historical demographics/conditions/geography only; not a licence-holder source for current Part 5 purposes. No current annual report file name was verified on 31 Oct 2026 — [Verify].

5.5 Department of Revenue (ADOR) — sales and tax reports (aggregate only, NO holder list)

ADOR Marijuana Tax Collection landing page (current month hub). URL: ADOR Marijuana Tax Collection [Directly opened 31 Oct 2026]. Heading on opened page: “September 2026 Reports” — with three PDF links and note: “Data is updated monthly. Source: Arizona Department of Revenue, Office of Economic Research and Analysis.” Holder types: none — tax data by category and jurisdiction, not by business. ADOR does not publish a list of TPT/MET licence holders. Format: PDF only (see below). No CSV/XLSX/dashboard/API found on this page. No export button beyond downloading the PDFs themselves.

ADOR direct downloads — EXACT links resolved from the landing page on 31 Oct 2026. All three are PDFs. The browser tool mis-labelled their titles in extraction; URLs below are the resolved download URLs from the landing page’s outlinks — use URLs, not tool’s title labels:

  1. Collections and Taxable Sales by Period Covered (PDF) — direct download: MJ_byPeriodCovered.pdf — file MJ_byPeriodCovered.pdf. Older mirror/superseded path found in search: OERA_MJ_byPeriodCovered.pdf — do not cite both as separate sources; /document/ path is current landing-page link.
  2. Collections / Distribution by Jurisdiction Type (PDF) — direct download: MJ_byJurisdictionType.pdf — file MJ_byJurisdictionType.pdf. Older path found in search: MJ_2023_11_byJurisdictionType.pdf.
  3. Total Collections (PDF) — direct download: MJ_byTotalCollectionsCategory.pdf — file MJ_byTotalCollectionsCategory.pdf.

Fields — by Period Covered: for each period covered (transaction month, NOT receipt month): Total Tax Collections (all jurisdictions) and Taxable Sales (Estimated), split as Medical-203 (medical TPT), Adult Use-420 (adult-use TPT), Excise Tax-920 (16% marijuana excise tax), Fees-180. Footnote (as extracted): “Taxable Sales is estimated based on revenue received. The Taxable Sales for each period covered will change as late returns, late payments, amendments and audits are processed.” and “Period Covered is the month in which the transaction occurred, and is different than the month the tax revenue was received by ADOR.” Fields — by Jurisdiction Type: collections by month received, split State General Fund, Education Tax/Extension, County Revenue Sharing, City Revenue Sharing, Marijuana Excise Tax, EFT Payment, Total Marijuana Taxes; plus county/city tax detail. Fields — by Total Collections Category: total collections by category (medical TPT, adult-use TPT, excise, fees) by month received, with fiscal-year totals (FY2022–FY2027 columns in current file). Update frequency as stated: monthly (“Data is updated monthly”). Latest data date found: landing page headed September 2026; by-period file’s month columns run through Sep-26 in extracted text, and by-jurisdiction file’s monthly totals run through Sep 2026. Do not quote a single September 2026 total from the garbled text extraction — download the PDFs and read the tables visually; late returns/amendments revise earlier months. Limitations — critical: no business-level data: ADOR files contain no dispensary/establishment names, licence numbers, or per-holder sales. A holder-level tax list does not exist publicly. Two different bases that must not be mixed: “by Period Covered” (transaction month, estimated taxable sales) vs “by Jurisdiction Type / by Category” (month received). Summing across the two bases double-counts. Negative/revised months occur: e.g. third-party reporting of ADOR data notes a negative medical collection month in July 2026 (approx. -$556,500) reflecting adjustments — normal for this series and why ADOR footnotes revisions. Taxable sales are estimates, not audited sales: derived from tax received, not from ADHS transaction weights.

ADOR guidance — Adult Use Marijuana / TPT (licensing-to-tax bridge, not a holder list). URL: ADOR adult-use FAQ [found via search]. Content: seller must first hold an ADHS licence, then apply to ADOR for TPT/MET registration (Form JTM-1, Medical/Adult Use/Dual License Transaction Privilege/Use/Excise Tax Application); medical dispensaries subject to state/local retail TPT; adult-use subject to retail TPT plus 16% Marijuana Excise Tax reported on Form MET, not Form TPT-2; dual-use establishments file both. Format: web FAQ. No holder list, no download of registrants. Limitations: ADOR TPT/MET registration numbers are not published. ADHS licence number and ADOR TPT/MET numbers are different identifiers — do not conflate them.

Secondary tax summaries (NOT primary holder sources — cite only as cross-checks): Arizona Joint Legislative Budget Committee (JLBC), Monthly Fiscal Highlights — e.g. March 2026: JLBC Monthly Fiscal Highlights Mar 2026 (PDF) — contains a “Marijuana State Tax Collections and Distributions” table ($ millions). Format: PDF. Fiscal summary, not a licence source. ADOR TPT rate tables (state/county/city TPT rates applicable to marijuana sales) — [Verify current table URL on azdor.gov; no rate-table file was directly opened on 31 Oct 2026, so no file name is given here].

Other counts — and why they conflict:

  1. Testing labs: 8 (ADHS, 1 Aug 2026) vs 7 (CannBus Q3 2026, “certified by ADHS, down from 21 in December 2022”). UNRESOLVED CONFLICT — do not pick one. Possible explanations (none proven): different cut-off dates within Aug 2026; “certified” vs “total labs” definitions; a lab closing/losing certification between ADHS count and CannBus compilation; or a CannBus counting error. [Verify current lab count from AZCareCheck’s Marijuana Facilities list in a live browser] before publishing a single number. Lab-agent figure conflict (107 July vs 98 Aug per CannBus) is a second, smaller conflict on same point.
  2. Social equity: 26 licences awarded (see §4.4). No separate social-equity holder file was found. Social-equity status must be inferred from 2022 lottery reporting + AZCareCheck/establishment records, and later transfers/partnerships (reported by AZCIR/press: majority of original winners no longer holding equity) mean the 2022 winner list is not a current social-equity holder list. [Verify current social-equity operating status holder-by-holder; do not republish the 2022 list as current.]
  3. Rural county licences: 13 licences awarded Apr 2021 (see §4.2) — included in 170 total, not additional. Third-party summary saying “12 rural county licences” (IndicaOnline) vs 13 in contemporaneous MJBizDaily — use 13, flag 12 as error/different counting basis [Verify].
  4. Pharmacy-ratio cap: see §4.1 — 136 DRCs vs 1,323 pharmacies (Fennemore secondary summary of Dec 2025 ADHS audit) does not arithmetically yield current stock. [Verify Dec 2025 ADHS allocation notice itself; Fennemore summary is secondary.]
  5. Third-party directory counts — DO NOT USE as licence counts: Sativa University directory (third party, undated in extract): 204 dispensaries statewide, with city splits (Phoenix 57, Tucson 26, Mesa 17, etc.). This counts storefronts/directory listings, including dual medical + adult-use locations counted in ways ADHS licences are not, and is not an official source. Americans for Safe Access state page (third party): 137 medical retail locations, 89,666 patients — patient figure conflicts with ADHS July 2026 (70,444) and is undated/differently defined. Do not use either third-party figure in place of ADHS. Rule: ADHS monthly report = licence counts; AZCareCheck = named adult-use/lab records; directories = location-finding aids only, never counts.
  6. Cultivation/manufacturing site counts: No current official site count was found. Only site figure is third-party summary citing ADHS/JLBC figure of 111 cultivation sites for FY2023 as “latest published” [Verify — CannBus citing JLBC fiscal note for SB 1641]. 2024 Citation Report gives licences cited by site type, not total sites. Do not publish a current cultivation-site total; mark as Unavailable from public sources found.
  7. Delivery licensees: NO COUNT EXISTS because there is no separate delivery licence (see §4.6). Delivery activity is not separately enumerated in monthly report or AZCareCheck instructions found. [Verify whether any ADHS delivery-activity field exists in AZCareCheck detail pages.]

5.7 Department of Agriculture (AZDA) — hemp licence data sources

5.7.1 AZDA Industrial Hemp Program — FAQ / programme hub. URL: AZDA Industrial Hemp FAQ [Directly opened 31 Oct 2026]. Holder types (licence types, for context only — Part 7 covers types): Grower, Nursery, Harvester, Transporter, Processor (A.A.C. R3-4-1003; A.R.S. § 3-314). Format: web FAQ page. No licensee list, no CSV, no roster table on this page. Access: free. Page states licences are required, there is no cap on number of licences and no acreage minimum/maximum, and applications may be made at any time; post-processed hemp/finished-goods retail does not require an AZDA licence. Update frequency/latest date: not stated. Fields: none as holder data. Limitations: proves programme exists and its rules; does not enumerate holders.

5.7.2 AZDA Industrial Hemp Program — application forms (the ONLY AZDA hemp downloads verified). Direct download — application (10 pages, form dated 06/22/22 in extract): HempApplication.pdf — file HempApplication.pdf. Fields on form: applicant (business or sole proprietor — name to be printed on licence), licence type(s) selected (Grower/Harvester/Transporter/Processor/Nursery), business information, background, growing/storage locations and maps. Historical note: a 2019 version of the form included a “Step 3: Information for Public Posting” section stating information would be “posted to the Department’s website … once the applicant is issued a license” — but no such current public posting/roster page was found on agriculture.az.gov on 31 Oct 2026. Whether AZDA still posts licensee information, and where, is [Verify — ask AZDA / public records request; do not assume 2019 form wording is current practice]. Direct download — programme rules: AZDA_Title_3_Ch_4_Art_10.pdf — file AZDA_Title_3_Ch_4_Art_10.pdf (A.A.C. Title 3, Chapter 4, Article 10, Industrial Hemp — R3-4-1001 to R3-4-1012, including fee table R3-4-1005). Direct download — FAQ PDF (older, 12 Mar 2024 version found in search): Hemp FAQ 03-12-24.pdf — file Hemp FAQ 03-12-24.pdf. Key statement in that FAQ: “Yes, the USDA approved Arizona’s State Hemp Plan for implementation on January 1, 2022.” Research licensing page: AZDA hemp research licensing [found via search] — covers not-for-profit research fee exemption under R3-4-1004; no roster. Limitations — critical: No AZDA hemp licensee roster, CSV, or searchable database was found. Historical counts exist only in third-party summaries (see §5.7.3) and in individual licensees’ own published licence certificates (e.g. a 2026 Grower/Nursery/Processor certificate set with licence numbers in the AZ-G/AZ-N/AZ-P series, expiring 31 Dec 2026, found on a licensee’s own site — not an AZDA publication and not a roster). State this gap plainly: current AZDA hemp holder counts are Unavailable from public sources found; route is a public-records request to AZDA, not a download.

5.7.3 Historical AZDA hemp counts (third-party summary only — NOT current). From a third-party industry summary (hempcbdbusinessplans.com, updated 14 Apr 2024, citing AZDA programme history — [Verify against AZDA directly]): 2020: 157 grower licences + 60 processor licences; acreage 1,130 acres (down from ~5,430 acres in 2019). 2021: 196 licences total (88 grower, 24 nursery, 13 harvester, 29 transporter, 42 processor). 2022: 92 licences total (38 grower, 12 nursery, 5 harvester, 14 transporter, 23 processor). As of Feb 2024: 92 licences total in that summary’s table (18 grower, 6 nursery, 3 harvester, 9 transporter, 12 processor — note these sum to 48, not 92, so summary’s own table is internally inconsistent and must not be quoted without verification). Do not present any of these as 2026 counts. See Part 6, §6.7 for the same series and its break after 2022.

5.8 Arizona Corporation Commission (ACC) — business entity search (not a cannabis licence source)

Current portal (third-party reports, Jan 2026): Arizona Business Center, at arizonabusinesscenter.azcc.gov/businesssearch as cited in third-party guides [Verify exact URL on azcc.gov itself — no official ACC search page was directly opened on 31 Oct 2026]. Former portal: eCorp (ecorp.azcc.gov/entitysearch/index in older guides). Third-party reports state eCorp was decommissioned at 5 p.m. MST on 2 Jan 2026 and Arizona Business Center went live 12 Jan 2026, with old address redirecting — [Verify against an official ACC announcement before publishing migration dates]. Holder types: LLCs, corporations, partnerships, nonprofits — all Arizona entities, not cannabis-specific. A cannabis business appears here only as a business entity, with no cannabis licence status. Format: interactive search only. No bulk download, no cannabis filter, no export button documented for public search. Access: free public search; CAPTCHA (“I’m not a robot”) verification required before results, per third-party guides. Search fields: Entity name, Entity ID, Statutory Agent name, Principal name; filters: Starts With/Contains/Exact Match, entity type (domestic/foreign), status (active/inactive), name type, county. Fields on a record: current status, principal address, statutory agent, managers/principals, filing history and filed documents. Update frequency: live filing system; no stated snapshot date. Limitations — critical: ACC status ≠ cannabis licence status. An entity can be ACC-active but have no ADHS licence, a lapsed ADHS licence, or an ADHS licence under a different legal name/DBA. Arizona LLCs file no annual report (only corporations do), so ACC “active” is a weak currency signal for LLCs. DBAs/trade names are NOT in ACC (see §5.8.1) — a DBA-only search in ACC will miss the trading name. Sole proprietorships are not ACC entities at all.

5.8.1 Secretary of State — Trade Names (DBAs) — companion search, not a licence source. Agency: Arizona Secretary of State (SOS), Business Services — Trade Names & Trademarks. SOS handles trade names, trademarks, UCC, notary and partnerships; ACC handles entities. URL: azsos.gov trade-names pages [exact search URL not verified — third-party guides cite a truncated https://azsos.gov/business/trade-names-t… URL; do not cite a truncated URL — Verify live SOS trade-name search URL]. Format: interactive search/online filing. No bulk download documented. Fields: registered trade name, registrant, 5-year term, $10 filing fee (third-party guides; [Verify current fee]). Limitations: trade-name registration in Arizona is optional and is not a cannabis licence; many businesses trade under unregistered names. County Recorder fictitious-name filings for unincorporated businesses are a further, county-level layer — [Verify county-by-county; no statewide index was found].

5.9 Tribal licence records

5.9.1 Tribal marijuana licences — NO public register found; NO tribal-state compacts found. Finding: no public tribal marijuana licence register, tribal dispensary list, or tribal-state cannabis compact was found in official sources on 31 Oct 2026. Third-party summary (cannabisinarizona.org, “last verified: March 2026” — [Verify, not official]): cannabisinarizona.org tribal cannabis — states Arizona has 22 federally recognised tribes each setting its own cannabis policy; Navajo Nation maintains strict prohibition (including hemp, per its 2020 code expansion); Salt River Pima-Maricopa Indian Community legalised adult possession (up to 1 oz, 21+) by a 4–3 council vote in 2022 but had not opened a tribal dispensary as of March 2026; Gila River Indian Community maintains prohibition; and “No formal compacts: No tribal-state cannabis compacts exist in Arizona.” Limitations: tribal law applies on tribal land, not Arizona state law, so ADHS/AZCareCheck coverage does not extend to tribal-licensed activity (if any). Any tribal holder data would have to come from the individual tribal government — none was found published. Do not infer tribal holders from ADHS lists, and do not list tribal dispensaries from commercial directories (Leafly “Indigenous-owned” filters etc.) as licensed tribal holders — those are marketing filters, not licence records.

5.9.2 Tribal HEMP plans (USDA) — plans, not holder lists. USDA plan-review page: USDA plan-review page [Directly opened 31 Oct 2026; page stated “Updated February 17, 2026”]. Arizona tribal plans listed: San Carlos Apache Tribe of Arizona — Approved Plan (pdf) (no asterisk) and Tonto Apache Tribe of Arizona — Approved Plan (pdf) * (approved under final rule effective 22 Mar 2021). Format: PDF plan documents, linked from plan-review table. Text extraction did not expose direct PDF file names/URLs for the two tribal plans or for the Arizona state plan — download via plan links on that page in a live browser; do not guess file names. Limitations: a USDA-approved plan is not a holder list. It authorises the tribe to license hemp producers under its own plan; tribe’s own licensee records, if public, were not found.

5.10 USDA hemp records for Arizona

5.10.1 USDA AMS — State and Tribal Hemp Plan status (authoritative plan source). URL: USDA plan-review page [Directly opened 31 Oct 2026]. Arizona state entry: “Arizona | AZ Approved Plan (pdf)*” — i.e. Arizona operates under its own USDA-approved state plan (final-rule approval), not under a USDA Hemp Producer Licence. Consistent with AZDA FAQ statement that plan was approved for implementation 1 Jan 2022 (see §5.7.2). Format: web table with PDF plan links. No holder list, no CSV. Update frequency: page stated “Updated February 17, 2026” on opened version. Fields: plan holder (state/tribe/territory), status (Approved / Approved * / USDA Hemp Producer Licence), plan PDF link. Limitations: proves who licenses Arizona hemp (AZDA under its state plan, plus two tribal plans in §5.9.2), not who is licensed.

5.10.2 USDA HeMP (Hemp eManagement Platform) — public vs law-enforcement search (access asymmetry). HeMP information page: USDA HeMP page [found via search]. HeMP is the secure system for USDA producers, states, tribes, testing labs and sampling agents to manage licences and mandatory reporting. Use requires USDA eAuthentication (eAuth) or Login.gov account with identity verification. Public search tool (third-party citation, NOT directly opened): https://hemp.ams.usda.gov/s/PublicSearchTool as cited in a Kansas state portal page describing USDA’s search tool for “basic information on licensed hemp producers across the country” — [Verify this URL and its Arizona coverage in a live browser; do not cite it as verified until opened]. Expected fields (if tool works as described for other states): entity name, licence number (USDA or state/tribe format), county/growing lot, licence status. For Arizona, coverage is expected to be via AZDA state-plan data reported into HeMP, not via USDA-issued licences — [Verify whether Arizona state-plan producers are searchable in the public tool at all]. Law-enforcement search tool (NOT public): https://hempsearch.ams.usda.gov — requires eAuth credentials and prior access request to USDA (farmbill.hemp@usda.gov, per HeMP Law Enforcement Search Tool User Guide: Law_Enforcement_Search_Tool_User_Guide.pdf). Search fields include city, growing-lot county, entity name, licence number, holder first/last name (USDA programmes only), EIN. File name: Law_Enforcement_Search_Tool_User_Guide.pdf. Limitations — critical: public tool, if it covers Arizona at all, is not a substitute for an AZDA roster, and its completeness for state-plan producers is explicitly caveated by USDA (“tool may not capture all approved hemp growing locations” in law-enforcement guide). No Arizona-specific USDA producer count was found in sources opened. Do not import another state’s HeMP count methodology into Arizona. USDA Farm Service Agency (FSA) lot registration (growers/nurseries must register crops with USDA-FSA, per AZDA FAQ) is a separate dataset from licensing and was not found as a public holder list.

5.11 Local county / city / town permit or zoning records

General finding: No Arizona city in the required set publishes a single, consolidated, downloadable “cannabis licence list.” What exists is (a) a zoning/use-permit process in code, and (b) case-by-case approvals in council/planning agendas or permit portals. Mesa is the closest exception (a registration + map). Counties: no county-wide cannabis licence register was found; counties act mainly through zoning for unincorporated areas and through city-level processes for incorporated places — [Verify Maricopa/Pima/other county unincorporated-area permit portals separately if needed; none was verified on 31 Oct 2026].

(3) Permit/plan-review search: Tucson PRO portal (pro.tucsonaz.gov) — searchable by project/address, case-by-case (e.g. pre-application reviews for a proposed dispensary).No Tucson dispensary roster. Special Exception process means each new/relocated dispensary generates a council file — only way to enumerate locally is to search PRO + council files. Old Tucson fact-sheet figures (2,500 sq ft cap, 9 a.m.–7 p.m. hours, 2,000 ft dispensary separation) are superseded in part (current UDC text cites 7 a.m.–10 p.m. hours in later summaries) — [Verify current UDC § 4.9.9.E text before quoting dimensions].
MesaCLOSEST TO YES — registration + map, but no downloadable list verifiedMarijuana Facility Registration Process page: Mesa Marijuana Facility Registration [found via search] — states every medical dispensary, dual-licensee facility, cultivation facility and infusion facility desiring to operate in Mesa must register with Mesa’s Planning Division; facilities permitted in Light Industrial (LI) and General Industrial (GI) districts under Zoning Ordinance § 11-31-34; applications via DIMES portal (Administrative Review → Marijuana Facility Registration); on approval Planning Director signs a Registration Certificate and staff place a permanent marker on the Marijuana Facility map. Sept 2025 Mesa newsletter (Mesa newsletter bulletin) notes amendments increasing dispensary/dual-licensee size from 2,500 to 5,000 sq ft and removing medical-only home-delivery restriction.Map itself was not downloaded / its direct URL was not extracted — [Verify live map URL and whether it is exportable]. Mesa also historically banned recreational-only establishments and testing facilities (Ord. 5601, reported 2020–2021) — current code position after Sept 2025 amendments must be [Verified against current Mesa Code Ch. 25 / § 11-31-34] before stating what Mesa now permits. No CSV of registrants found.
ChandlerNOZoning Code Ch. 35, § 35-2213 (medical marijuana facility/cultivation site/infusion food establishment). History via council agendas (DestinyHosted): 2011 Ord. 4278 (use permit); 2017 Ord. 4764 (replaced use permit with administrative zoning clearance); 2023 Ord. 5044 (expanded hours/size, allowed collocated ancillary facility in I-1/I-2 via Conditional Use Permit, 1-year expiry); 2025 Ord. 5127 (replaced 1-year condition with any period approved by Council) — direct download: 8249_25.04.25_Ordinance_5127_Dispensary_City_Code_Amendment.pdf — file 8249_25.04.25_Ordinance_5127_Dispensary_City_Code_Amendment.pdf. Individual CUP files and safety plans are in Scottsdale eServices document viewer, case-by-case (e.g. Public Safety Plan for a Scottsdale dispensary, doc viewer link: Scottsdale eServices doc viewer). Jan 2026 Planning Commission report on a CUP renewal (9-UP-2015, 14980 N 78th Way) confirms 2021 amendment (6-TA-2020) allowing recreational uses at facilities also licensed for medical use. Scottsdale historically banned recreational-only establishments (reported 2020–2021) — [Verify current position].No Scottsdale roster; eServices search is by document/case, not a cannabis list.

County note: no county in Arizona was found to publish a cannabis-specific licence register. For unincorporated areas, relevant local record is county zoning/use-permit file (e.g. Maricopa County, which 2011 summary listed among jurisdictions requiring a UP/CUP/SUP — [Verify current Maricopa County position; do not rely on 2011]). If county coverage is needed, it must be built from county planning portals case-by-case and labelled as such.

5.12 Other / secondary sources (use only as finding aids, never as licence counts)

  • Cannlytics open cannabis-licence dataset documentation: Cannlytics cannabis licences dataset — lists Arizona’s source as https://azcarecheck.azdhs.gov/s/?licenseType=null (i.e. it scrapes AZCareCheck). Format: Hugging Face dataset, partitioned by state (az). Limitations: third-party scrape, undated snapshot, inherits all AZCareCheck limitations (§5.2) plus scrape error. Useful only to show that no other official Arizona source exists in that dataset’s methodology.
  • Commercial directories (Weedmaps, Leafly, Sativa University, ArizonaStateCannabis.org, etc.): location/menu/delivery finding aids. They list storefronts, not licences, and their counts (e.g. 204, 137) conflict with ADHS for reasons in §5.6. Do not cite them for counts, licence status, or tribal status.
  • Metrc (seed-to-sale tracking): referenced in third-party summaries as Arizona’s mandatory tracking system [Verify — no Metrc public holder list exists; Metrc is a licensee-only system, not a public register, and must not be listed as a public data source].

5.13 Bottom line for Part 5

The only complete, current, official, named-holder source for Arizona marijuana is AZCareCheck (adult-use + labs, search-only, daily); the only complete official count source is the ADHS Monthly Report (July 2026 / 1 Aug 2026: 170 establishments / 169 operating, 136 medical dispensaries / 136 operating, 8 labs); the only official tax sources are the three ADOR PDFs listed in §5.5; and for hemp, tribal marijuana, social-equity-as-a-separate-file, delivery-as-a-separate-file, and local consolidated lists (except Mesa’s registration + map), the honest Part 5 finding is no public downloadable register found — with records-request / case-by-case routes given above.

PART 6: MARKET DATA

6.0 How to read this — method, and what was NOT directly opened

  • Directly opened in this research: ADHS Arizona Marijuana Program June 2026 Monthly Report; ADHS July 2026 report via search extract; ADHS December 2024 and December 2025 reports via search extracts; Arizona Department of Revenue (ADOR) Marijuana Tax Collection landing page; CannBus Arizona Cannabis Market Intelligence Report Q3 2026 (data as of 30 Sep 2026, itself a compilation of ADOR/ADHS); Headset Arizona market page (snapshot 3 Oct 2026); AZDA Industrial Hemp FAQ page; AZDA hemp licence lists dated 21 Jun 2022 and 14 Feb 2024 (PDFs found, full tallies not extracted — see §6.7).
  • ADOR PDF contents were NOT extracted in full text in this research. ADOR landing page, opened 31 Oct 2026, lists “September 2026 Reports” — Collections and Taxable Sales by Period Covered, Collections/Distribution by Jurisdiction Type, Total Collections — but PDF tables themselves did not render as text. Every ADOR sales/tax figure below is therefore via a secondary compilation of ADOR (CannBus, ArizonaStateCannabis, Marijuana Herald, Headset/Impact Databank), labelled as such, unless it is a tax-collection total corroborated by two compilations. Mark for parent verification: pull the ADOR September 2026 “by Period Covered” PDF directly before publishing any 2026 monthly sales table.
  • ADOR sales are estimates, not till totals. ADOR footnotes, as quoted by Cannabis Data Project (accessed 31 Oct 2026): “Taxable Sales is estimated based on revenue received. The Taxable Sales for each period covered will change as late returns, late payments, amendments and audits are processed.” Period-covered = month of transaction; month-received = month ADOR received the money. The two bases differ materially (see §6.1.4). One month’s medical figure can go negative when amendments/audits exceed filings — e.g. July 2026 medical TPT collections ≈ −$556,500 (Marijuana Herald, 12 Aug 2026, citing ADOR).
  • No personal phone numbers or personal emails are reproduced. Only contact reproduced is ADOR’s published agency address/phone from its own landing page, and that is omitted here as non-market data except for the source link.
  • Official sources used: ADHS June 2026 Monthly Report: ADHS June 2026 (PDF) — accessed 31 Oct 2026; ADHS July 2026 Monthly Report: ADHS July 2026 (PDF) — accessed 31 Oct 2026 (via search extract); ADHS December 2025 Monthly Report: ADHS Dec 2025 (PDF) — accessed 31 Oct 2026; ADHS December 2024 Monthly Report: ADHS Dec 2024 (PDF) — accessed 31 Oct 2026; ADOR Marijuana Tax Collection page: ADOR Marijuana Tax Collection — opened 31 Oct 2026; states adult-use = TPT + 16% excise on retail sales; CannBus Q3 2026: CannBus Q3 2026 — opened 31 Oct 2026; data as of 30 Sep 2026; Headset Arizona: Headset Arizona — opened 31 Oct 2026; snapshot 3 Oct 2026; AZDA Industrial Hemp FAQ: AZDA Hemp FAQ — accessed 31 Oct 2026; AZDA licence list 14 Feb 2024: AZ-HempLicenses-02-14-24.pdf — accessed 31 Oct 2026.

6.1 Sales — medical and adult-use, by year and by month

6.1.1 Tax structure (official, ADOR/ADHS via CannBus). Adult-use: 16% marijuana excise tax (Form MET-1) + state Transaction Privilege Tax (TPT) 5.6% for retail + county/city TPT. ADOR business codes: adult-use retail 420, medical retail 203, excise 920. Medical: retail TPT only, no 16% excise. CannBus-calculated average combined adult-use TPT ≈ 8.39% in 2025 ($89.28m adult-use TPT ÷ $1,064.67m taxable sales — note denominator differs slightly from $1,058.7m excise-base figure in §6.1.2; both are CannBus calculations, not ADOR-published rates). Effective total tax ≈ 22.3% overall in 2025, ≈ 24.4% on adult-use (CannBus calc., collections ÷ taxable sales).

6.1.2 Annual taxable sales — PREFERRED series (CannBus sums of ADOR monthly “by Period Covered” estimates). CannBus sums adult-use = ADOR excise-base (category 920); medical = category 203. ADOR revises monthly estimates as late returns arrive, so earlier press totals differ (see reconciliation).

Calendar yearAdult-useMedicalTotalYoY total

Derived shares: medical was 23.8% of total in 2023 ($347.3m ÷ $1,462.0m), 18.5% in 2024, 13.5% in 2025. Medical fell −34.1% in 2025; adult-use fell −4.3% in 2025. Spending per medical patient in 2025 ≈ $2,035 ($165.3m ÷ 81,221 patients at Dec 2025; CannBus calc., slightly understated by a negative Aug 2025 adjustment).

6.1.3 Annual totals — CONFLICTING series (reconciliation required). Do not publish a single annual series without noting these conflicts; they reflect different ADOR vintages, different inclusion rules, or different (POS vs tax-estimate) universes, and no source in this research explains the full gap:

  • ArizonaStateCannabis.org “Arizona Marijuana Sales Report 2026” (secondary compilation citing ADOR; page updated 6 Oct 2026 in opened version, 8 Sep 2026 in search version): 2021 $733,222,781; 2022 $1,425,590,598; 2023 $1,376,423,972; 2024 $1,250,468,715; 2025 $1,184,317,044. That is $39.7m below CannBus for 2025, $106.9m below for 2024, $85.6m below for 2023. Its 2021 total is not comparable — its monthly table shows “No Data” Jan–Jun 2021 and only Jul–Dec 2021 summed, despite adult-use sales having begun 22 Jan 2021 — so 2021 figure is a partial-year artefact, not a full-year total. Verify.
  • Headset / Impact Databank (via Shanken News Daily, 21 Apr 2026, reporting 2025 full-year POS-tracked sales): Arizona 2024 $1,303.6m; 2025 $1,210.5m, −7.1%. Close to CannBus ($1,224.0m) but not identical; Headset tracks a POS panel, ADOR estimates from tax receipts, so a gap is expected. Headset’s 12 months ending Sep 2026: Arizona $1.22bn, −0.5% YoY (Headset industry-statistics page, updated 3 Oct 2026, secondary).
  • MJBizDaily, 18 Apr 2025 (via CannBus source list): $1.26bn for 2024 from ADOR data at the time; current ADOR table sums to $1,357.4m (CannBus). This is a vintage/revision difference, not an error by either source — ADOR revises. Preferred figure for 2024 is latest-vintage $1,357.4m, with $1.26bn figure noted as superseded.
  • Older secondary narrative totals (for context only, do not use as official): AZMarijuana (secondary, undated in extract but reporting 2023): 2023 recreational ≈ $1.1bn + medical $348m = “more than $1.4bn”, third consecutive year above $1.4bn; since Jan 2021 to end-2023, recreational $2.8bn, medical “slightly more than half that” at $1.65bn. A separate secondary blog (Purple Rose Supply, citing Associated Press 2024) gives yet another split — 2021 medical $733m / recreational $953m / total $1.68bn; 2023 medical $284m / recreational $1.14bn; 2024 medical $243m / recreational $1.06bn / total $1.3bn — inconsistent with both CannBus and ArizonaStateCannabis, and its 2021 medical figure ($733m) exactly equals ArizonaStateCannabis’s 2021 “total”, suggesting a mislabelled series. Treat as [VERIFY — do not publish]. The $733m figure also appears in ArizonaStateCannabis 2021 total, reinforcing that at least one of these series is mislabelled.
  • ResearchAndMarkets forecast (secondary, commercial forecast, method opaque): Arizona legal cannabis market “projected to reach USD 1,740.2 million by 2030, registering a CAGR of 17.3% from 2023 to 2030” (report page accessed 31 Oct 2026). This contradicts observed 2023–2025 decline in every ADOR-derived series. Flag as low-reliability forecast; do not present alongside ADOR actuals without that warning. Emergen Research’s “Arizona” page (accessed 31 Oct 2026) is unusable for Arizona: its headline $32.50bn (2025) / $71.50bn (2035) figures are explicitly U.S.-market figures mislabelled as Arizona. Do not cite.
  • Bottom line: use CannBus/ADOR-vintage series in §6.1.2 as working series, footnote ArizonaStateCannabis and Headset alternatives, and flag that a direct ADOR PDF pull is still needed to close the $40–107m annual gaps.

Aug 2026 $296,856 is not a plausible monthly total (prior Aug months $94–111m; Headset Sep 2026 alone is $95.2m). It matches ADOR’s practice, noted by Cannabis Data Project, of printing a row of dollar signs/zeros for a month whose returns have not yet been processed, here partially populated. Exclude Aug 2026 from any total; Jan–Jul 2026 combined on this series = $710,023,693. Note also that this series’ Jan–Jul 2026 combined ($710.0m) minus CannBus adult-use Jan–Jul 2026 ($627.4m) would imply medical Jan–Jul 2026 ≈ $82.6m — but that subtraction mixes two different ADOR vintages/compilations and is not presented as a verified medical monthly series.

6.1.6 Monthly medical sales — NOT FOUND as a separate monthly table. No separate month-by-month medical taxable-sales table was found in any directly opened source in this research. What exists: annual medical splits in §6.1.2 (CannBus/ADOR); secondary narrative points (all [VERIFY], from AZMarijuana / Marijuana Moment reporting of ADOR): medical peaked at $73.4m in April 2021; fell below $40m in July 2022 and “has not come near that mark” since; Oct 2022 ≈ $31.4m (Sep 2022 $32.5m); Dec 2023 $25.4m; Jan 2024 $21.5m — described as consecutive record lows at the time. Adult-use in same narratives: $80–93.5m/month Jul 2022–Mar 2023, $100m in Mar 2023, then mid-to-low $80m range; Jan 2024 adult-use $76.8m, lowest since Feb 2022 ($72.8m) and first sub-$80m month since Jun 2022. Headset POS monthly totals (combined, not split): Sep 2026 $95.2m (+5.2% YoY, units +8.8% YoY); Aug 2026 $100.31m (+5.0% YoY, units +8.5% YoY, via CannBus/Headset); six months ending Jun 2026 $619.9m vs $637.6m in H1 2025, −2.8% (Headset/Impact Databank via Shanken News Daily, 13 Oct 2026). Headset’s Sep 2026 growth (+5.2%) sits uneasily against ADOR-derived flat-to-down 2026 — different universes (POS panel vs tax estimates) and different months; do not splice them into one trend line.

6.1.7 Tax collections (official concept, secondary-compiled figures). ADOR counts collections by month received (different from period-covered sales in §6.1.4–6.1.5). “Total Collections” = state + county + city + education TPT + marijuana excise tax.

  • Jan–Jun 2026: $149,549,586 (+2.3% vs $146,181,586 in Jan–Jun 2025). Breakdown H1 2026: 16% excise $93.18m; adult-use TPT $49.89m; medical TPT $6.47m (Marijuana Herald, 10 Jul 2026, citing ADOR). Monthly 2026: Jan $24.98m; Feb $25.25m; Mar $23.58m; Apr $23.56m; May $26.49m (highest since May 2023’s $28.4m, and +6.8% vs May 2025 $24.8m); Jun $25.69m (nearly +8% vs Jun 2025). Implied combined sales from tax rates ≈ $658m in H1 2026 vs ≈ $655m in H1 2025 (+0.4%) — Marijuana Herald estimate method: excise ÷ 16% for adult-use, adult-use TPT for average TPT rate for medical; local rates and prior-period adjustments affect estimate. Label as estimate, not ADOR sales.
  • Jan–Jul 2026: $173,452,328 (+2.4% vs $169,349,815 in Jan–Jul 2025). July 2026 alone $23,902,742: excise ≈ $14.99m; adult-use TPT ≈ $9.47m; medical TPT ≈ −$556,500 (adjustments). Jan–Jul 2026 breakdown: excise ≈ $108.2m; adult-use TPT ≈ $59.4m; medical TPT ≈ $5.9m (Marijuana Herald, 12 Aug 2026, citing ADOR). Cumulative marijuana tax since adult-use launch: “nearly $1.5bn” by Jul 2026 (same source); $1.44bn by May 2026; $1.37bn by Feb 2026 — consistent progression, not three different totals.
  • State fiscal years (Jul–Jun, by month received): FY2025 $283,565,186; FY2026 $281,961,540 (Motley Fool state table, citing ADOR 2026, accessed 31 Oct 2026 — secondary). CannBus gives FY2026 $282.0m (−0.6% vs FY2025 $283.6m), excise FY2026 $175.1m (FY2025 $177.8m) — reconciled: $0.04m gap between Fool and CannBus FY2026 is rounding/vintage, immaterial. By period covered, CannBus gives FY2026 collections $271m in one summary (Herring Bank, citing ADOR) vs $282.0m by month received — that $11m gap is the period-covered vs month-received timing difference, explicitly flagged by CannBus §4: same H1 months total $139.8m by period covered vs $149.5m by month received. Do not mix bases.
  • Calendar 2025 adult-use-only tax (MPP, secondary, adult-use focus): Arizona $246,722,756 in 2025 (Marijuana Policy Project analysis via Marijuana Moment, 2026 — excludes medical, fees, income tax, local tax). Not comparable to $282m FY total above.
  • Monthly collections “have not fallen below $20m since October 2021” (Marijuana Herald, 14 Mar 2026, citing ADOR) — useful floor, secondary.

6.2 Decline of the medical market and patient counts since Prop 207

6.2.1 Patient counts — official ADHS point-in-time series (qualifying patients only). ADHS monthly reports, Tables 1–4 = point-in-time active cardholders. “Qualifying Patients” ≠ “Total Cardholders” (total adds caregivers, dispensary agents, facility agents, lab agents). Do not conflate them — several secondary pages do.

Decline arithmetic (calc. from above): Jul 2021 peak 313,906 → Aug 2026 69,144 = −244,762 patients, −78.0%. Dec 2022 → Aug 2026 = −46.7% (CannBus states −37.4% for Dec 2022→Aug 2026 using a different base — CannBus’s −37.4% does not reproduce from 129,836→69,144; its −14.9% for Dec 2025→Aug 2026 does reproduce: 81,221→69,144 = −14.9%. Flag −37.4% as [VERIFY — likely a typo or different base in CannBus].) Dec 2025→Aug 2026 −14.9% in 8 months is the fastest recent leg; June 2026 report alone shows a sharp step (May→Jun) that coincides with the two-year card cycle — see §6.2.3. A secondary page (ArizonaStateCannabis medical statistics) prints a monthly table whose Jan–May 2026 values (96,744; 95,197; 93,075; 91,341; 89,414) match ADHS total cardholders, not qualifying patients, while its Jun–Aug 2026 values (71,605; 70,258; 69,144) match qualifying patients — that table mixes two different ADHS columns and must not be cited as a patient series without correction. Its Jul 2026 patient figure (70,258) also differs from ADHS Jul report (70,444) by 186 — minor, but another reason to use ADHS PDFs directly.

6.2.2 Caregivers, cultivation, SNAP, agents (ADHS, directly opened / extracted). Designated caregivers: Jun 2026 155 (58 cultivation-approved); Jul 2026 156 (58); Feb 2026 190 (78); Aug 2026 146 (CannBus). Collapse from ~9,489 dispensary agents pre-facility-agent era is structural, not demand: Prop 207 created the cheaper, flexible facility agent card, and ADHS itself notes dispensary-agent cards fell as facility-agent cards rose (Arizona Mirror, Nov 2022, quoting ADHS spokesman Tom Herrmann). Cultivation-approved qualifying patients: Jun 2026 473; Jul 2026 442; Feb 2026 626; Apr 2026 537; Dec 2025 687; Dec 2024 821. Total cultivation-approved (all card types) Jun 2026 531; Jul 2026 500; Feb 2026 704; Apr 2026 606; Dec 2025 769; Dec 2024 914. Long-run context: in Apr 2011–Jun 2012, >80% of patients/caregivers were cultivation-authorised (ADHS First Annual Report, via secondary) — authorised-home-grow share has collapsed as retail access expanded. Do not compare 2011–12 percentage directly to 2026 counts without noting denominator change. SNAP-eligible patients: Jun 2026 6,782; Jul 2026 6,600; Feb 2026 8,144; Apr 2026 7,359; Dec 2025 8,621; Dec 2024 9,632. Facility agents: Jun 2026 16,105; Jul 2026 16,061; Aug 2026 16,040 (CannBus; Dec 2025 16,792, −4.5% to Aug 2026). Dispensary agents: 2 in every 2026 report opened — category effectively extinct. Laboratory agents: Jun 2026 108; Jul 2026 107; Aug 2026 98 (CannBus; Dec 2025 108). Applications (ADHS Jun 2026, directly opened): Jun 2026 new patient applications 1,584, renewals 2,335, total patient applications 3,980; YTD 2026 new 9,127, renewals 16,259, total 25,819 patient applications. Facility-agent applications Jun 2026: 387 new + 206 renewals. Revocations Jun 2026: 5 in month, 18 YTD. Physicians certifying in Jun 2026: 172 in month, 430 YTD. Aug 2026 certifying physicians: 142 in month, 479 YTD (CannBus). Renewals still exceed new applications roughly 1.8:1 YTD — programme is shrinking by attrition, not by collapse in new entry alone. County concentration (ADHS Jun 2026, directly opened): Maricopa County 44,849 of 71,605 patients (62.6%); Pima 12,057 (16.8%); Pinal 5,429; Yavapai 3,104; all other counties ≤1,164 each; outside Arizona 179. Herring Bank’s “about 63% live in Maricopa County” (Jul 2026 report summary) is consistent.

6.2.3 Qualifying conditions (ADHS Jun 2026, directly opened; patients can list multiple conditions, so shares sum >100%). Chronic pain 66,942 (93.49%); PTSD 2,551 (3.56%); cancer 1,672 (2.34%); nausea 1,618 (2.26%); muscle spasms 1,467 (2.05%); seizures 662 (0.92%); glaucoma 476 (0.66%); Crohn’s disease 380 (0.53%); HIV/AIDS 331 (0.46%); hepatitis C 126 (0.18%); cachexia 96 (0.13%); Alzheimer’s 32 (0.04%); sclerosis 28 (0.04%). Aug 2026 (CannBus): chronic pain 93.41%, PTSD 3.66%, nausea 2.34%, cancer 2.33%, muscle spasms 2.05% — essentially unchanged. Age (Jun 2026): 18–30: 11,043; 31–40: 12,014; 41–50: 14,078; 51–60: 12,094; 61–70: 13,408; 71–80: 8,078; 81+: 856; <18: 34. Sex: female 32,554, male 39,051.

6.2.4 Medical volume (ADHS, medical dispensaries only — no adult-use weight is published). Jun 2026 (directly opened): month 54,411.31 oz total (marijuana 45,181.22 oz; edibles 1,415.08 oz; other 7,815.02 oz) = 3,400.71 lb. YTD Jan–Jun 2026: 350,430.70 oz = 21,901.92 lb. Full-year (CannBus calc. from ADHS): 2025 781,470 oz, −10.1% vs 2024 (869,153 oz). Note tension: volume fell only −10.1% while medical taxable sales fell −34.1% in 2025 — implying sharp fall in taxable value per ounce (price compression, mix shift, and/or negative Aug 2025 tax adjustment CannBus flags). Do not present volume and sales declines as if they should match; flag divergence. Historical secondary points: Jan 2021 medical purchases 9,273 lb in ~600,000 transactions (Beard Bros summary of ADHS, 2022 — “nearly halved to 5,061 lb” point in that article is garbled and mixes months; use only Jan 2021 anchor with [VERIFY]); Nov 2022 5,606 lb (4,814 lb flower + remainder edibles/other); Jan 2021 (alternate secondary) 18,708 lb — two Jan 2021 pound figures (9,273 vs 18,708) conflict directly; one likely counts a different product universe or period. [VERIFY — do not publish either without ADHS Jan 2021 PDF.] Feb 2024: 4,754 lb in month; Jan–Feb 2024 YTD 9,562 lb (AZMarijuana, secondary). Why patients left — causes as reported, not as official ADHS findings: ADHS does not publish an attrition-cause survey in reports opened. Secondary explanations, all to be labelled as such: (a) adult-use availability from 22 Jan 2021 removed need for a card for most consumers (Arizona Mirror, 2022, via secondary); (b) SB 1494 (signed Jun 2019) extended card validity from one year to two years, depressing renewal counts and fund revenue (Auditor-General update, via secondary) — this also creates two-year echo effects in monthly series; (c) certification costs ($75–$150 card fee + physician fees, per Arizona Mirror 2022) vs no card cost for adult-use, partially offset by medical’s exemption from 16% excise tax and higher purchase limits (2.5 oz / 14 days vs 1 oz adult-use). No source in this research quantifies each cause’s share. [UNAVAILABLE — causal split.]

Historical: Dec 2022 — 169 adult-use establishments licensed, 140 operating (CannBus citing ADHS Dec 2022). Licensed count barely moved (169→170) while operating count rose 140→169 between Dec 2022 and mid-2026 — i.e. post-2022 story is build-out of already-licensed sites, not new licences, consistent with one-per-10-pharmacies cap (A.R.S. §36-2854) plus 26 social-equity licences. Testing labs: ADHS July 2026 report gives 8 labs as of 1 August 2026. The CannBus August 2026 figure of 7 certified labs is secondary only (no ADHS August 2026 report was opened in this audit) and is not adopted as the ADHS count. The earlier series 21 (Dec 2022) → 18 → 15 remains secondary and Verify against ADHS/JLBC source documents.

6.3.2 Licence classes — what does NOT exist (official, ADHS fee schedule / 9 A.A.C. 18 via CannBus). No separate wholesale, transport, delivery, microbusiness, or consumption-lounge licence. Delivery is allowed under establishment licence (R9-18-312.01: orders in posted business hours, ID/age verification at handover, $10,000 retail value cap per vehicle trip, tracked vehicle with video, no delivery to government property; Prop 207 barred delivery until after 1 Jan 2023). Number of establishments actually offering delivery: N/A — not published. Cultivation and manufacturing are sites under an establishment licence, not standalone licences: $2,500 to add a cultivation or manufacturing site; establishment fees $25,000 initial application, $2,500 approval to operate, $5,000 biennial renewal (licences are valid 2 years under §36-2854(A)(1)(g)); testing facility $25,000 initial / $5,000 renewal; facility agent $300 ($150 with Level 1 clearance card) (CannBus citing ADHS fee schedule — secondary compilation of official fees; verify fee schedule before republication — Part 4 fees above are from directly opened AAC text and should be preferred). Cultivation sites: 111 (FY2023) — latest published figure found, from JLBC fiscal note for SB 1641 via CannBus. No 2024–2026 cultivation-site or manufacturing-site count was found. [UNAVAILABLE for 2024–2026.] Pending: SB 1641 would create standalone marijuana producer licences from 1 Jan 2029 (Senate passed 23–5 on 11 Mar 2026; House Commerce Committee 7–3 on 24 Mar 2026; final outcome not confirmed in CannBus Q3 2026 — Part 4 confirms it died at sine die 13 June 2026; do not present as enacted).

6.3.3 Social equity — awarded vs operating (gap to reconcile). See full detail in §4.4. Awarded (official): 26 licences, live-streamed random drawing 8 Apr 2022 from “more than 1,500 applications” (CannBus citing ADHS) — AZCIR reports “more than 1,300 applicants” for same drawing (see §4.4: 1,301 in drawing vs >1,500 filed). Reconcile: 1,300 vs 1,500 gap is unexplained in sources opened; ADHS’s own figure should be checked on ADHS Social Equity page. [VERIFY exact applicant count.] Application cost $4,000 (CannBus; AZCIR reported no fee waiver in 2023). Eligibility: principal officer/board member with ≥51% ownership meeting at least 3 of 4 criteria (low household income <400% poverty level for 3 of 5 years; prior Arizona marijuana conviction; family member with one; residence in a heavily affected area / one of 87 qualifying ZIP codes for ≥3 years 2016–2020 — criteria wording varies slightly by source; ADHS page is authoritative). ADHS ran no loan or grant fund with programme (CannBus). Operating count — OFFICIAL: UNAVAILABLE. ADHS monthly reports publish total establishments (170/169) but do not break out how many of those hold a social-equity-origin licence, nor how many social-equity licences are operating vs licensed-but-not-operating. No ADHS social-equity operating list was found. Any “X social-equity dispensaries operating” figure must be labelled secondary/dated, not official. Secondary timeline is in §4.4 (13 opened late 2023, 1 independent; Feb 2024 hearing claim 24/26 corporate-controlled; internally inconsistent 4+13+10=27 split in same AZCIR update).

6.4 Official price data

Explicit finding: ADHS and ADOR publish NO retail price series for Arizona cannabis. Neither ADHS monthly reports (which publish medical volume in ounces, not dollars per ounce) nor ADOR tax tables (which publish estimated taxable sales, not prices) contain a price per gram/ounce/eighth. Cannabis Data Project (accessed 31 Oct 2026) confirms derivation problem: ADHS medical weight blends marijuana, edible and “other” ounces (an edible’s ounces are product weight, not cannabis weight), covers medical channel only, and ADOR medical dollars go negative on amendments — so no defensible official price-per-gram can be derived from official data. Do not substitute secondary prices as “official” anywhere.

6.4.1 Secondary prices (all labelled secondary, with dates — and they conflict).

  • Headset, Sep 2026 (snapshot 3 Oct 2026, directly opened): average item price $17.05 (vs $17.64 a year prior); flower $5.94/g, flower eighth $14.12, edible (100 mg) $9.47, concentrate $13.03/g; top strain Kush Mints; Sep 2026 total sales $95.18m, units 5.6m, +5.2% YoY sales, +8.8% units.
  • Headset via CannBus, Aug 2026 (snapshot 3 Sep 2026): average flower price $10.28/g and $14.46 per eighth (+4.2% YoY); total Aug sales $100.31m (+5.0% YoY, units +8.5% YoY); category split §6.5. Aug flower gram ($10.28) vs Sep flower gram ($5.94) conflict is stark and unexplained — different Headset definitions (e.g. “average flower price” vs “flower (gram)” list price), different retailer panels, or a data error. Present both with dates and conflict flag; do not average them.
  • Marijuana Herald, 14 Oct 2026 (secondary, citing September Headset + state-agency data): Arizona average flower price $4.03/g (3rd cheapest of 16 states with verifiable data, behind Michigan and Oregon), average concentrate $13.03/g — concentrate matches Headset Sep exactly, flower does not match either Headset figure ($5.94 or $10.28). Three-way flower-price conflict: $4.03 vs $5.94 vs $10.28 per gram, all ostensibly September/August 2026 Headset-derived. [VERIFY — Headset methodology note required before any price claim.]
  • Older secondary ranges (for trend context only): May 2025 Marijuana Herald/Headset: Arizona ≈ $8/g flower. Elevate Holistics (published 7 May 2025, updated 11 Sep 2025, “statewide averages 2024–2025”, method unstated): flower 1 g medical $12–$18 / recreational $8–$25; 1/8 oz $28–$50; 1/2 oz $85–$180; 1 oz $99–$280; vape 0.5 g $17–$42; concentrates 0.5 g $25–$40, 1 g $15–$80; single edible $4–$10; tincture bottle $20–$140 — wide, unsourced ranges; use only as “retail menu ranges reported in 2025”, not as market averages. MMJ.com (reviewed Jul 2026, dispensary menus): flower $3.57–$14.29/g, average $9/g; eighths $20–$50; edibles $8–$22 per 100 mg; vape carts $10–$68 — same caveat. Bluntness (Jan 2026, citing state agencies/Headset/Cannabis Business Times): Arizona gram $8–$12, eighth $35–$50, ounce $150–$250 — inconsistent with Headset’s $14.12 eighth; ounce figures in menu guides largely reflect half-ounce bulk deals, not standard ounce pricing (MMJ.com notes full 28 g packages are uncommon on Arizona menus; half-ounce $24–$50 deals dominate).
  • Price-compression signal (more robust than any single price): every secondary source agrees on direction — Headset Sep 2026 average item price down YoY ($17.64→$17.05) while units rose (+8.8%); CannBus/Headset Aug 2026 units +8.5% vs dollars +5.0%; medical volume down only −10.1% while medical dollars down −34.1% (§6.2.4). Market is selling more units for fewer dollars — price compression is well-supported even though level of price is not.

6.5 Market concentration among operators

Official finding: ADHS and ADOR do not publish market share by operator, by brand, or by store. CannBus states this explicitly (§11). No HHI, CR4/CR10, or operator revenue share can be computed from official data. [UNAVAILABLE — official concentration.] What official structure implies (not a share estimate): market is structurally concentrated by design — hard cap near 170 vertically integrated licences (one per 10 pharmacies + 26 social equity), mandatory vertical integration (each establishment licence covers cultivation + manufacturing + retail), and no microbusiness tier. Secondary commentary (Drug Policy Watch Arizona brief, 2026, accessed 31 Oct 2026) characterises this as “heavily fenced” and favouring well-capitalised MSOs over small/independent entrants. That is editorial characterisation, not a measured share.

Secondary operator/brand signals (dated, no shares attached): Arizona Dispensaries Association members (2026, via CannBus): Curaleaf, Trulieve, The Mint Cannabis, Sol Flower, Zen Leaf (Verano), JARS Cannabis, Nature Med, Story Cannabis, The Flower Shop, Sunday Goods. This is a membership list, not a ranking and not a share table. Verano store count (secondary, Aug 2024): 8 Arizona retail locations after acquiring two Cannabist stores (Tempe, Prescott) in Aug 2024 (Cannabist Company release via CannBus). Dated; current Verano Arizona store count [VERIFY]. Headset brand ranking, Aug 2026 (secondary, no percentages published in opened sources): leading consumer brands by Aug 2026 sales — STIIIZY, Jeeter, Wyld, Select, Find (CannBus §11; Headset Sep page lists Jeeter, STIIIZY, Wyld, Select, Find, Gron in a different order with no shares). Order differs between two Headset-derived lists; treat as “top brands named”, not a ranked share. Headset category split, Aug 2026 (secondary, $100.31m total): flower $36.0m (35.9%); vapor pens $31.6m (31.5%); pre-rolls $16.6m (16.5%); edibles $8.8m (8.8%); concentrates $5.6m (5.6%); beverages & other remainder $1.7m. Vapor pens +13.3% YoY, beverages +18.2% YoY (CannBus). Sep 2026 Headset page (directly opened) gives a different category cut — flower $34.47m, vape $30.13m, pre-roll $15.57m, edibles $8.27m, concentrates $5.23m, beverages $1.08m + smaller segments — totalling $95.18m, consistent with Sep total but with slightly different category labels. Beverages Sep: +20.5% sales, +30.7% units YoY; sports/energy beverages +135.4% sales, +203.6% units YoY; leading beverage brands Keef Cola ($0.32m), Sip Elixirs ($0.29m), Uncle Arnie’s ($0.10m) in Sep 2026. Stale MSO store counts — do NOT present as current: MJBizDaily, Nov 2021 (secondary): Trulieve 16 Arizona stores + 320,000 sq ft cultivation/processing + 3 additional licences + option on a 20th (after acquiring Harvest Health & Recreation); Curaleaf 9 stores; Verano 6 stores. These predate social-equity build-out and multiple acquisitions; current per-operator store counts [UNAVAILABLE in opened sources]. Derived averages (calc., not concentration): 2025 adult-use taxable sales $1,058.7m ÷ 169 operating establishments ≈ $6.26m per operating establishment (CannBus calc.); ≈ 2.8 operating establishments per 100,000 adults (169 ÷ (7,623,818 × 79.0% adults) × 100,000; Census QuickFacts Jul 2025 population via CannBus). These describe scale, not concentration.

6.6 Employment as a market proxy (supplementary, not asked but material to market size)

  • Official proxy: active facility-agent cards — 16,105 (Jun 2026), 16,061 (Jul 2026), 16,040 (Aug 2026); lab-agent cards 108 → 98 over same span. These are cardholder counts, not FTE headcount (one person can hold one card; cards lapse; not all cardholders are employed full-time).
  • Secondary: Vangst 2026 U.S. Cannabis Jobs Report (23 Jun 2026) via CannBus: Arizona 25,200 FTE, methodology described as Vangst-based and “not re-verified” by CannBus. National jobs 412,500 (early 2026, −2.7% YoY). Label Vangst as secondary/modelled; do not reconcile FTE to card counts.

6.7 Hemp — acreage and licence counts by year (AZDA / USDA)

Regulator and plan: Arizona Department of Agriculture (AZDA), Industrial Hemp Program (Environmental Services / Plant Services Division). Licence types: Grower, Nursery, Harvester, Transporter, Processor (five types). Grower fee $1,000; Processor $2,000 (plus per-ton assessments for processors; grower inspection $25/outdoor acre to 100 acres then $5/acre, $75/indoor facility to 3 acres then $25/acre — via Sativa University compilation of AZDA rules, secondary). No cap on number of licences or acreage size (AZDA FAQ, directly accessed). Applications accepted any time; licences expire 31 Dec (annual, with two-year renewal option per secondary). USDA approved Arizona’s State Hemp Plan 28 Dec 2021, effective 1 Jan 2022 (secondary, hempcbdbusinessplans, citing USDA; USDA AMS approved-plans list, updated 17 Feb 2026, confirms “Arizona — AZ Approved Plan” — USDA plan-review page). Hemp = Cannabis sativa L. ≤0.3% total delta-9 THC dry weight (AZDA FAQ).

Under-protection area 3.52m sq ft (+9%). Survey: 8,153 producers nationally (Feb–Mar 2025). Sources: USDA NASS via Cannabis Science & Tech (17 Apr 2025 report coverage), Hemp Gazette (21 Apr 2025), CBD.market (14 May 2025) — all secondary summaries; USDA report itself was not directly opened. Gross value per acre (derived, Hemp.com Jun 2026 summary of USDA 2024): floral ≈ $32,600/acre, grain ≈ $540/acre, fibre ≈ $594/acre (derived $11.2m ÷ 18,855 acres), blended open-field ≈ $12,800/acre — gross production value before seed, labour, drying, testing, hauling, marketing costs; not profit, not Arizona-specific.

6.8 Hemp product market figures

Official Arizona hemp-product market figure: NONE FOUND. AZDA publishes no hemp sales, wholesale-price, or retail-market series on its FAQ or licence pages found in this research, and USDA’s National Hemp Report value figures are national, not Arizona-broken-out in sources opened. [UNAVAILABLE — official Arizona hemp product market size.] Secondary / contextual only: National hemp-derived product context (not Arizona): national $445m (2024) USDA production value in §6.7.3 is farm-gate production value, not retail CBD/hemp-product sales. Retail CBD market estimates were not located for Arizona specifically and are not invented here. Arizona intoxicating-hemp position (market-shaping, not a sales figure): Arizona Attorney General opinion (2024, via secondary summaries Nov 2025) treats delta-8/delta-10/THCA-type cannabinoids as controlled substances unless produced/sold within licensed marijuana (dispensary) system — i.e. no lawful general-retail intoxicating-hemp market in Arizona. AG enforcement deadline 24 Apr 2025 to cease sales of certain THC-infused products or face penalties (FundCanna secondary, citing AG action). DoorDash hemp-THC/CBD delivery in select Phoenix areas (Cannabis Cactus secondary, 2025) sits in tension with that position and is not evidence of a lawful general-retail channel. No sales figure for Arizona hemp-THC products exists in lawful channels to report; any such figure would be illicit-market estimation and is not attempted. Hemp greens/microgreens: from 2022, Arizona growers may commercially produce hemp microgreens/greens compliant with state/federal rules (secondary, citing USDA plan approval). No sales figure found. [UNAVAILABLE.] Combined marijuana+hemp forecasts (ResearchAndMarkets $1,740.2m by 2030, §6.1.3) include hemp in their “source” split but do not publish Arizona hemp-only component in excerpt opened. [UNAVAILABLE as a separate figure.]

6.10 Industry events and trade shows in Arizona

No official (ADHS/ADOR/AZDA) events calendar was found. All events below are secondary-sourced, dated, and — where organiser page was not opened — flagged. No 2025–2026 edition of former flagship Southwest Cannabis Conference & Expo in Phoenix was verified; do not list it as a current recurring event on basis of its 2016–2017 editions.
